# **ChainTorrent MVP**

## Problem Statement

JavaScript dependency distribution runs through a single centralized registry that is an availability chokepoint, a mutable source of truth, and an intermediary between the people who publish packages and the people who consume them. ChainTorrent's MVP replaces that path with a content-addressed swarm, a canonical on-chain identity registry, and transferable access entitlements, so that the distribution and identity model is proven with monetization out of bounds.

**Adoption runs individual developer first, and everything else follows from that.** A developer at a terminal gains cross-project reuse of whatever their machine has already fetched, swarm retrieval on popular packages, and installs that survive a registry outage. Build platforms adopt next and for their own reasons: a service running the same few thousand popular installs continuously, on always-on machines with real bandwidth, holds the ideal cache as a by-product of its own economics, and seeding that cache costs almost nothing once it exists. That makes them natural superseeders rather than participants anyone has to recruit, and it is why no privileged bootstrap host is part of this plan. CI and enterprise adoption arrive as a consequence of that sequence rather than as targets to be won early.

The sequence matters to this workplan because it determines what the MVP measures. Latency is judged against interactive local installs rather than pipeline tolerance, since a developer notices a doubled install where a pipeline does not. Cache reuse is the headline adoption metric, because it is the benefit the adopting population actually experiences. Registry-outage survival remains true throughout and is simply not the lead, since it matters most to the population that adopts last.

The design is specified in `docs/research/cryptography.md` and bounded in `docs/research/MVP Scope.md`. Undetermined decisions are held in the To-Do list below.

The cryptographic construction is specified; its research record is `docs/research/cryptography-research-notebook.md`, and its statement is `docs/research/cryptography-critical-path.md`. A validation harness on both pairing curves produces the sizes, timings, and gas against the configured piece-group size and curve defaults, and nothing waits on it: every node reads the piece-group size from the deployment record and depends only on the code it calls.

## Objectives

Deliver an install path for JavaScript dependencies that survives registry outages, serves popular packages from a peer swarm, and exercises the identity and entitlement pipeline end to end.

Prove distribution and identity. Willingness to pay and seeder compensation are out of bounds and are held in the To-Do list; the transaction flow is proven at a nominal price on assets the project publishes, as a test instrument rather than a monetization step.

Every undetermined decision is carried in the To-Do list, which every workplan that stems from this one inherits.

## Expected Outcome

A developer installs dependencies and receives a working `node_modules` assembled from a local content-addressed store, a peer swarm, or the ingest source as fallback, with canonical identity resolved on-chain and an access entitlement minted to their wallet.

## Delivery proof and on-chain verifier

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/delivery/mint` **The mint delivery, generic over the credential KEM, the key agreement, and the delivery proof with its chain forms: over a typed mint context and an admitted identity, a parameter set set up under the identity's setup scope, the identity's element, a credential issued under the master scalar, the recipient's key pair generated and its public keys admitted with their proof of possession, the credential wrapped to them, and the mint statement proved; returns the statement and proof, the parameter set, identity element, and credential, the witnesses the prover took, the recipient as a holder, and the uniforms it drew; owns the admitted identity, the holder, the mint delivery, and the mint witnesses; creates the `delivery` module**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: every harness consumer that needs a mint, the proof vectors, the statement mutations, the replay vectors, and the measurement, needs the same construction, setup, identity derivation, issuance, the recipient's keys, the wrap, and the mint proof, and the measurement times `prove_mint` over fresh witnesses, which no consumer can obtain once a prover has consumed them; one function owns the construction and returns the statement, the proof, the objects it was built from, and a copy of every witness the prover took (CD-01 delivery at mint, with its witnesses held for every consumer that proves, mutates, encodes, or times it; the critical path's mint proof, knowledge of `(α, r, ρ, σ)` with `hpub = g2^α`, `C1 = g1^ρ`, `C2 = g1^α · F_I^r · pk1^ρ`, `D1 = g2^σ`, and `D2 = g2^r · pk2^σ`; `docs/research/cryptography.md`'s Credential Delivery boundary and Phase 1's Minting, every mint delivering the first credential; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/delivery/mint` row; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the function sets up a parameter set from three uniforms under the admitted identity's setup scope, derives the admitted identity's element, issues a credential from a fourth uniform, generates the recipient's key pair from a fifth and a sixth and admits its public keys with their proof of possession, wraps the credential to them, and proves the mint statement over a clone of the mint context, the parameter set's `hpub`, the identity element, the recipient's public keys as the recipient keys, and the envelope
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the delivery holds the statement and the proof; the parameter set, the identity element, and the issued credential; the mint witnesses, the master scalar's components, the issuance randomness, and the wrap's coins, each a copy of the value the prover took; the recipient as a holder of its key pair, public keys, and envelope; and the six uniforms concatenated in draw order
    * `[✅]`   Functional: every uniform has the length the pairing's scalar sampling bound declares; the envelope coins and the proof nonces are drawn inside their adapters and are not part of the draws
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a failed draw, setup, derivation, issuance, key generation, key admission, wrap, or proof is returned unchanged in the error arm
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the delivery proof's context is cloneable, so one admitted context serves any number of statements; the KEM's setup scope and identity are copyable, so one admitted identity serves any number of calls
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the function reads no clock and touches no filesystem; it names no curve, concrete, form, scope, identity, purpose, or context value; the context and the identity arrive in its payload

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/delivery/transfer` **The transfer delivery, generic over the credential KEM, the key agreement, and the delivery proof with its chain forms: over a typed transfer context, the parameter set, the identity element, and the holder it transfers from, the holder's envelope opened under the holder's key pair, the buyer's key pair generated and its public keys admitted with their proof of possession, the opened credential rerandomized, the result wrapped to the buyer's keys, and the transfer statement proved; returns the statement and proof, the rerandomized credential, the witnesses the prover took, the buyer as a holder valid for the next transfer, and the uniforms it drew; owns the transfer delivery and the transfer witnesses**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: every harness consumer that needs a transfer, the proof vectors, the statement mutations, the replay vectors, and the measurement, needs the same construction, the seller's fresh opening of its envelope, the buyer's keys, the rerandomization, the wrap, and the transfer proof, and the measurement times `prove_transfer` over fresh witnesses, which no consumer can obtain once a prover has consumed them; one function owns the construction from any holder and returns the statement, the proof, the credential it delivered, a copy of every witness the prover took, and the buyer as a holder the next transfer takes (CD-02 delivery at sale, the seller decrypting its own envelope afresh, rerandomizing with a private offset, encrypting to the buyer's registered keys, and posting the envelope with a transfer proof against the previous envelope, the seller's persisted offset never required; the critical path's transfer proof, knowledge of `(x_s, y_s, s, ρ, σ)` with `pk1_s = g1^x_s`, `C1_n = g1^ρ`, `C2_n / C2_o = C1_o^(-x_s) · F_I^s · pk1_b^ρ`, `pk2_s = g2^y_s`, `D1_n = g2^σ`, and `D2_n / D2_o = D1_o^(-y_s) · g2^s · pk2_b^σ`, only the total offset entering; `docs/research/cryptography.md`'s Phase 3 Delivery and its Delivery Proof transcript, the source entitlement equal to the target for a sale and the author's entitlement for a grant; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/delivery/transfer` row; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the function opens the holder's envelope under the holder's key pair, generates the buyer's key pair from a first and a second uniform and admits its public keys with their proof of possession, rerandomizes the opened credential under the payload's parameter set and identity element from a third uniform, wraps the rerandomized credential to the buyer's admitted keys, and proves the transfer statement over a clone of the transfer context, the identity element, the holder's public keys as the seller keys, the buyer's public keys as the buyer keys, the holder's envelope as the old envelope, and the new envelope
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the delivery holds the statement and the proof; the rerandomized credential; the transfer witnesses, the holder's key-pair components, the rerandomization offset, and the wrap's coins, each a copy of the value the prover took; the buyer as a holder of its key pair, admitted public keys, and new envelope, which a further transfer takes as its holder; and the three uniforms concatenated in draw order
    * `[✅]`   Functional: every uniform has the length the pairing's scalar sampling bound declares; the envelope coins and the proof nonces are drawn inside their adapters and are not part of the draws
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a failed draw, key generation, key admission, rerandomization, wrap, or proof is returned unchanged in the error arm
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the function reads no clock and touches no filesystem; it names no curve, concrete, form, scope, identity, purpose, or context value; the context, the parameter set, the identity element, and the holder arrive in its payload

    * `[✅]`   Functional: a library name is an ASCII uppercase letter followed by ASCII letters and digits; empty text, a leading byte outside `A`–`Z`, and the lowest later byte outside that set are each refused, the refusal carrying the byte and, for a later byte, its index
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a constant name is an ASCII uppercase letter followed by ASCII uppercase letters, digits, and underscores; empty text, a leading byte outside `A`–`Z`, and the lowest later byte outside that set are each refused, the refusal carrying the byte and, for a later byte, its index
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a type name, naming a record struct, is an ASCII uppercase letter followed by ASCII letters and digits; a member name, naming a struct member or a decode function, is an ASCII lowercase letter followed by ASCII letters and digits; for each, empty text, a leading byte outside its leading set, and the lowest later byte outside `A`–`Z`, `a`–`z`, and `0`–`9` are each refused, the refusal carrying the byte and, for a later byte, its index
    * `[✅]`   Functional: an ABI type is a validated name built from the type name its caller supplies, the text of the name the encoding family reports for a canonical field kind: an ASCII letter followed by ASCII letters and digits, the set an encoding's type names are drawn from; empty text, a leading byte outside `A`–`Z` and `a`–`z`, and the lowest later byte outside `A`–`Z`, `a`–`z`, and `0`–`9` are each refused, the refusal carrying the byte and, for a later byte, its index; the module owns no table from canonical field kinds to ABI type names
    * `[✅]`   Functional: `Text` and `Bytes` are the canonical field kinds a decoded local variable holds in `memory`; the module decides the data location from a member's kind and writes the member's type as the ABI type the member carries
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a record's members are an ordered list of at least two members, each a member name, a canonical field kind, and an ABI type, with unique names and none named `line`, the decode function's parameter; fewer than two members is refused with the count, at the lowest index whose name equals a name at a lower index the refusal carries the lowest such earlier index and that index, and a member named `line` is refused with its index
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a record declaration is a struct type name, the members, a decode function's member name, a path constant's constant name, and the path as a string value; a library's record declarations are a non-empty ordered list whose struct names, decode function names, and path constant names are each unique within the list; an empty list is refused, and for each of the three names, at the lowest index whose name equals one at a lower index, the refusal names which name and carries the lowest such earlier index and that index
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a license identifier is an SPDX short identifier of ASCII letters, digits, `.`, and `-`, with at most one `+` as its final byte; text whose identifier part is empty is refused as empty, and the lowest byte of the identifier part outside that set is refused with its index in the whole text

    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the function reads no adapter, draws no randomness, reads no clock, and touches no filesystem; it holds no literal for a license, a compiler version, a library, type, member, or constant name, a path, or an entry; the decode function's parameter `line` and its local and argument names are the rendering's own, and a member never takes the parameter's name

  * `[✅]`   `construction`
    * `[✅]`   Each validated type's `try_new` is its only producer; `SolidityCompilerVersion` is built from its fields; `render` is the only producer of `SoliditySourceText` in production code, and `build_solidity_source_text` in `mock.rs` constructs it for tests
    * `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/constants` builds each `SolidityLibraryEntry` from a `SolidityConstantName` it admits and a `SolidityConstantValue`, and each field kind's ABI type through `SolidityAbiType::try_new(SolidityAbiTypeConstructorParams { name })` over the type name its borrowed encoder returns for the kind
    * `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/generate/solidity` admits the constants library's entries through `SolidityLibraryEntries::try_new` and renders it with `SolidityLibraryBody::Constants`; for each kind its declaration names, it builds a `SolidityRecordDeclaration` from the struct, decode function, and path constant names its per-kind settings carry, the members from the kind's record description's member names and canonical field kinds, each with the `SolidityAbiType` it built from its borrowed encoder's type name for that kind, through `SolidityRecordMembers::try_new`, and the path of the file it wrote, admits the list through `SolidityRecordDeclarations::try_new`, and renders the vector-record library with `SolidityLibraryBody::Records`; it calls `render` once per library with the library name, license identifier, and compiler version it holds
    * `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/config` admits the configured license identifier through `SpdxLicenseIdentifier::try_new`, builds the `SolidityCompilerVersion`, and admits every configured library, type, member, and constant name through `SolidityLibraryName::try_new`, `SolidityTypeName::try_new`, `SolidityMemberName::try_new`, and `SolidityConstantName::try_new`

  * `[✅]`   `apps/harness-crypto/src/generate/solidity/render/mod.rs`
    * `[✅]`   Module declarations: `mod interface;`, `#[cfg(any(test, feature = "mocks"))] mod mock;`, `pub mod provides;`, `#[cfg(test)] mod test;`, and `#[cfg(test)] mod integration_test;`
    * `[✅]`   Inherent `impl` blocks with `pub fn try_new` for `SolidityLibraryName`, `SolidityConstantName`, `SolidityTypeName`, `SolidityMemberName`, `SpdxLicenseIdentifier`, `SolidityStringLiteral`, `SolidityUint256`, `SolidityAbiType`, `SolidityLibraryEntries`, `SolidityRecordMembers`, and `SolidityRecordDeclarations`, each realizing its branches in the interaction spec; `pub fn as_str(&self) -> &str` for `SolidityAbiType`; `pub fn decodes_into_memory(&self) -> bool` for `SolidityRecordMember`; and `pub fn as_str(&self) -> &str` for `SoliditySourceText`
    * `[✅]`   `pub fn render(_deps: &RenderDeps, params: RenderParams, payload: RenderPayload) -> RenderReturn`, assembling the text by `String::push_str`, each hex digit pair by `format!("{byte:02x}")`, and each decimal by `to_string()`, through exhaustive `match`es over `SolidityLibraryBody` and `SolidityConstantValue`, each member's type written from the `SolidityAbiType` the member carries and its data location from the member's `decodes_into_memory`; the module holds no table from a kind to a type name
    * `[✅]`   Imports this module's names from `interface`, `core::convert::Infallible`, and `CanonicalFieldKind` from `encoding`
    * `[✅]`   No other item

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/constants` **The constants library's entries and the cell's values record, generic over the selected pairing and the chain's forms: the identity mapping's, the proofs of possession's, and the delivery proof's challenge and weight domain tags read from their families' declarations; the delivery-statement version and the delivery purposes' codes; the mint and transfer transcripts' field sequences as ABI type names; the signed-intent form's entries read from the forms' declaration, the key-identifier domain tag and the intent domain's name and version, the intent struct's name, and the intent's member names; the nonce kind's, the expiry kind's, and each canonical field kind's type name read from the borrowed encoder; and the record the cell directory holds, the curve's name and the precompile encoding's text read from the pairing's declaration, the scalar field's order, the generators' precompile encodings, and whether the verifier has second-group arithmetic**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the Solidity verifier hashes under the same domain tags, recomputes transcripts with the same field sequence under the same delivery-statement version and purpose codes, reduces modulo the same scalar field order, and starts from the same generators as the Rust reference, and the suite's contracts derive the key identifier and the intent's typed data under the same declared tag, domain, struct, member names, and type names, so each of these values is read from the reference and never written by hand, the values the same for every cell emitted as named entries of the constants library the suite's contracts import and each cell's values emitted as the record the cell directory holds (CR-09 and CR-11 parity inputs and LC-13's key identifier and intent typed data; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/constants` row; the technical requirements' file tree for the generated directory; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the identity mapping's tag is read from the KEM declaration, the first- and second-group proofs of possession's tags from the key-agreement declaration, and the challenge and weight tags from the delivery-proof declaration, each emitted as a byte string
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the delivery-statement version the function is handed is emitted as a 16-bit value when the proof family's transcript descriptions serve it, and refused with the version otherwise
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each declared delivery purpose's code is emitted as a 16-bit value under its purpose's name, in the declared purposes' order
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the mint and transfer transcripts' field kinds, read from their descriptions over the forms in play, are each emitted as one string of ABI type names separated by commas, each name the type name the borrowed encoder returns for the kind, wrapped as the ABI type `render` validates; the function and `render` hold no table from kinds to names
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the signed-intent form's entries are read from the declaration of the forms it is handed — the key-identifier domain tag emitted as a byte string, and the intent domain's name and version, the intent struct's name, and the intent's member names each emitted as a string
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the nonce kind's, the expiry kind's, and each canonical field kind's type name is read from the borrowed encoder, wrapped as the ABI type `render` validates, and emitted as a string
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the scalar field's order, read from the pairing's reference trait as big-endian bytes, is a member of the cell's values record, and an order whose length is not 32 bytes is refused with its length
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the first- and second-group generators' precompile encodings are members of the cell's values record
    * `[✅]`   Functional: whether the verifier has second-group arithmetic, read from the pairing's declaration, is a member of the cell's values record
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the record's members, in order, are the curve's name, the precompile encoding's text, the scalar field's order, the two generators' precompile encodings, and whether the verifier has second-group arithmetic; the curve's name is the `Debug` text of `P::DECLARATION.curve` and the precompile encoding's text is the `Debug` text of `P::DECLARATION.precompile_encoding`, each read from the received pairing's declaration
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the entries are returned in the order the interaction spec lists them, beside the cell's values record
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the function reads no clock, draws no randomness, and touches no filesystem; it names no curve, concrete, or chain; every tag, version, code, kind, and member it returns is read from its producer

  * `[✅]`   `role`
    * `[✅]`   App module in the adapter role: a function the Solidity concrete of the harness's generate family owns, beside `render` beneath the concrete's module
    * `[✅]`   The first source file generic over the forms `chain/create_chain_forms` hands a consumer that reads a transcript description's encoding contract, so it carries the edit giving `IChainForms` the supertraits `core::fmt::Debug + PartialEq + Eq`, which the transcript descriptions' derived error types require of their forms
    * `[✅]`   The first source file that formats a declared curve, so it carries the edit giving `PairingCurve` `#[derive(Debug)]`, the derive the curve's `Debug` text is read through
    * `[✅]`   Reads each canonical field kind's ABI type name from the borrowed encoder's type name for the kind and wraps it through `SolidityAbiType::try_new(SolidityAbiTypeConstructorParams { name })`, which `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/render` owns; holds no table of its own
    * `[✅]`   Does not choose, construct, or admit an encoder; the caller borrows the encoder it obtained through the encoding factory and this function only reads type names from it
    * `[✅]`   Does not render, name, or write the library or the record's file, and does not encode the record; `harness-crypto/generate/solidity` passes the entries to `render` under the library name its params carry, encodes the record under `CellValuesDescription` through the borrowed encoder, and writes each result
    * `[✅]`   Does not construct a pairing, KEM, key agreement, delivery proof, or forms; `harness-crypto/main` obtains each through its factory and the generate concrete hands this function the pairing and the declarations
    * `[✅]`   Does not carry a commit

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/group_vectors` **The pairing library's group-operation and pairing-check vector records, generic over the selected pairing: first-group addition, first-group multiplication, and the pairing check on every curve, and second-group addition and both groups' multi-scalar multiplication where the pairing declares second-group arithmetic at the verifier; each record its drawn scalars, the precompile's input, and the reference's output; owns the record types and their canonical descriptions with their member names, and declares the per-kind vector count type and the multi-scalar term count type in the generate family's `factory` interface**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the Solidity pairing library agrees with the Rust reference bit for bit only if every group operation and pairing check it performs is proven against vectors the reference produced, read by Foundry tests from committed vector files, one encoded record per line; the established form for precompile vectors, in EIP-2537's reference vector files, execution-spec-tests, and go-ethereum's precompile fixtures, is a record holding the precompile's input bytes and the expected output, with the pairing check's vectors holding passing products built by bilinearity and failing products beside them, and multi-scalar multiplication taking any positive number of point-and-scalar pairs; each record here also carries the scalars it was drawn from, as Foundry's failure persistence records a run's inputs rather than its generator state (CR-10 on-chain vectors; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/group_vectors` row; the technical requirements' `<records>` and `<line>` entries for `contracts/evm/generated`; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope; EIP-196, EIP-197, and EIP-2537's input and output encodings)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a vector count and a multi-scalar term count are each a positive integer; zero is refused
    * `[✅]`   Functional: every scalar a record uses is drawn through the randomness family at the length the pairing's scalar sampling bound declares and sampled through that bound; a record's draws are each drawn scalar's encoding through the pairing's `encode_scalar`, concatenated in draw order
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a first-group addition record draws `a` and `b`; its input is the encodings of `a·g1` and `b·g1`, and its output is the encoding of their sum through `add_g1`
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a first-group multiplication record draws `a` and the multiplier `s`; its input is the encoding of `a·g1` followed by the encoding of `s`, and its output is the encoding of `(a·g1)·s` through `mul_g1`
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a pairing-check record draws `a` and `b`, and on a record at an odd index also `c`; its input is the encodings of the pairs `(a·g1, b·g2)` and `(t·g1, g2)`, each pair's first-group encoding followed by its second-group encoding, where `t` is `−(a·b)` at an even index and `c` at an odd index; its output is the precompile's 32-byte word, every byte zero except the last, which is `0x01` where `pairing_product_is_one` returns true and `0x00` otherwise
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a second-group addition record is the first-group addition record over `g2`, `add_g2`, and second-group encodings
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a multi-scalar multiplication record holds the configured number of terms; for each term it draws the base's scalar `a_i` and the multiplier `s_i`, its draws being `a_1`, `s_1`, `a_2`, `s_2`, and so on; its input is, per term in order, the encoding of `a_i·g` followed by the encoding of `s_i`; its output is the encoding of the sum through `msm_g1` or `msm_g2`, `g` being that group's generator
    * `[✅]`   Functional: every first-group kind and the pairing check hold exactly their configured counts on every pairing; the second-group kinds hold exactly their configured counts where the pairing declares `BothGroups`, and are absent where it declares `FirstGroupOnly`; second-group settings handed for a first-group-only pairing, and none handed for a both-groups pairing, are each refused before any draw
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a failed draw or a failed sampling is returned unchanged in the error arm
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each record type's canonical description maps the record to its fields in member order, rebuilds the record from its fields read through the encoding family's canonical-field reader, carrying the reader's refusal unchanged, and states its member names, `draws`, `input`, and `output`, in field order and nothing else about its members; `harness-crypto/generate/solidity` pairs each name with the description's field kind and the borrowed encoder's type name for that kind
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the function reads no clock and touches no filesystem; it names no curve, concrete, kind count, file, library, struct, or function name; every count and term count arrives in its params, and every encoding is the selected pairing's

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/rejection_vectors` **The decode-rejection vector records, an enumerated kind: one record per candidate encoding the pairing family defines, the outside-the-subgroup point of each source group where that group is not the whole curve and the scalar field's order as the non-canonical scalar, each with what it decodes into and its bytes; owns the record type, its decode-target vocabulary, and its canonical description with its member names**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the Solidity pairing library performs subgroup checks where the precompile does not and must refuse every encoding the pairing family defines as invalid, so its Foundry tests read those encodings from a committed vector file, one encoded record per line, and expect each to be refused; the established form for precompile failure vectors, in EIP-2537's `fail-*` reference files, is a file of inputs every one of which must fail, so the kind itself states the expected outcome and no record carries one; the candidates are the pairing family's own reference encodings, whose refusal by every decoder the pairing family's own tests prove, so every library of a curve yields the same file (CR-10 subgroup rejection and encoding edge cases on chain; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/rejection_vectors` row; the technical requirements' `<records>` entry for decode rejection; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope; `IPairingReference`'s outside-the-subgroup rule in `adapters/pairing/src/factory/interface.rs`)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the records are, in order, the first group's outside-the-subgroup encoding where `g1_outside_subgroup_encoding` returns one, the second group's outside-the-subgroup encoding from `g2_outside_subgroup_encoding`, and the scalar field's order's bytes from `scalar_field_order`; nothing else
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each record names its decode target, `G1`, `G2`, or `Scalar`, and holds the candidate's bytes unchanged as its input
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a failed outside-the-subgroup search is returned unchanged in the error arm
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a decode target admits exactly its names and refuses any other text with the text
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the record type's canonical description maps the record to its fields in member order, rebuilds the record from its fields read through the encoding family's canonical-field reader, carrying the reader's refusal unchanged and refusing an unknown target name with the refusal, and states its member names, `target` and `input`, in field order and nothing else about its members; `harness-crypto/generate/solidity` pairs each name with the description's field kind and the borrowed encoder's type name for that kind
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the function draws no randomness, reads no clock, touches no filesystem, and decodes nothing; it names no curve or concrete; every candidate is the selected pairing's

  * `[✅]`   `role`
    * `[✅]`   App module in the adapter role: a function the Solidity concrete of the harness's generate family owns, beside `render`, `constants`, and `group_vectors` beneath the concrete's module
    * `[✅]`   Owns the record type, the decode-target type, and the record's canonical description with its member names
    * `[✅]`   Does not decode a candidate; each decoder's refusal of the family's candidates is proven in the pairing family's own tests
    * `[✅]`   Does not encode a record, write a file, or render a library; `harness-crypto/generate/solidity` encodes each record through the encoding factory under its description, writes the vector file, and builds the kind's record declaration from the description's member names, its field kinds, and the borrowed encoder's type names
    * `[✅]`   Does not compute a candidate; the pairing family's reference trait supplies every one
    * `[✅]`   Does not construct a pairing; `harness-crypto/run` obtains it through the pairing factory and the generate concrete hands this function the borrowed adapter
    * `[✅]`   Does not carry a commit

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/mapping_vectors` **The hash-to-scalar vector records, a drawn kind, each drawn message mapped under every domain tag the constants library carries, and the identity-mapping vector records, a kind over configured inputs, each admitted identity mapped through the credential KEM under its own setup scope and read from the identity element's components; owns both record types and their canonical descriptions with their member names**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the Solidity verifier recomputes the identity mapping, the proofs of possession's challenges, and the delivery proof's challenge and weight as keccak256 under a domain tag reduced modulo the group order, so its Foundry tests read the reference's mappings from committed vector files; the established form for hash-to-field test vectors, in RFC 9380's appendix vectors, is a domain separation tag, a message, and the resulting field element, every message taken under the suite's tag, so mapping one message under every tag the contracts hash under proves each tag is bound; the identity mapping is proven through the KEM itself, the scalar read from the identity element it derives, so the vector is what the issuer and the contract both compute (CR-08 and CR-11 identity-mapping and hash-to-scalar vectors; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/mapping_vectors` row; the technical requirements' `<records>` entries for hash-to-scalar and identity mapping; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the domain tags are, in order, the identity mapping's from the KEM's declaration, the first- and second-group proofs of possession's from the key-agreement declaration, and the delivery proof's challenge and weight from the delivery-proof declaration, each admitted as a `DomainTag`
    * `[✅]`   Functional: for each of the configured count of messages, the function draws a message of the configured length through the randomness family and emits one hash-to-scalar record per tag in tag order, each holding the tag's bytes, the message, and the 32-byte big-endian encoding of the scalar `hash_to_scalar` returns for that tag and message
    * `[✅]`   Functional: for each admitted identity the payload carries, in order, the function emits one identity-mapping record holding the identity's bytes, the canonical bytes of the entitlement form or the hash's bytes of the asset form, and the 32-byte big-endian encoding of the scalar of the identity element `derive_identity` returns for that identity, read through `identity_element_components`; each parameter set is set up under the identity's own setup scope
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each parameter set is set up from three uniform draws of the length the pairing's scalar sampling bound declares; its draws do not reach the record, since the identity element's scalar is a function of the tag and the bytes alone
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a refused tag, a failed draw, and a failed hash, setup, or derivation are each returned unchanged in the error arm
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each record type's canonical description maps the record to its fields in member order, rebuilds the record from its fields read through the encoding family's canonical-field reader, carrying the reader's refusal unchanged, and states its member names in field order and nothing else about its members: `tag`, `message`, and `scalar` for the hash-to-scalar record, and `identity` and `scalar` for the identity-mapping record; `harness-crypto/generate/solidity` pairs each name with the description's field kind and the borrowed encoder's type name for that kind
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the function reads no clock and touches no filesystem; it names no curve, concrete, tag, count, length, scope, or identity, and admits no identity; every tag is read from a declaration, every count and length arrives in its params, and every identity arrives in its payload admitted, paired with its setup scope

  * `[✅]`   `role`

    * `[✅]`   Owns the hash-to-scalar and identity-mapping record types and their canonical descriptions with their member names; consumes `VectorCount`, which the generate family's `factory` interface owns and `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/group_vectors` declares there
    * `[✅]`   Does not encode a record, write a file, or render a library; `harness-crypto/generate/solidity` encodes each record through the encoding factory under its description, writes the vector files, and builds each kind's record declaration from the description's member names, its field kinds, and the borrowed encoder's type names
    * `[✅]`   Does not choose a count, a message length, a scope, or an identity, and does not admit an identity; the configuration carries the count and the length, every identity arrives admitted and paired with its setup scope, and `harness-crypto/generate/solidity` passes them here
    * `[✅]`   Does not construct a pairing, hash-to-scalar adapter, KEM, or randomness source; `harness-crypto/run` and its consumers obtain each through its factory and the generate concrete hands this function the borrowed adapters and the declarations
    * `[✅]`   Does not carry a commit

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/possession_vectors` **The possession vector records, a drawn kind: envelope key pairs generated through the key-agreement family from secret draws the function makes, each record carrying the drawn secrets, the public keys, and the proofs of possession's components, the proofs' nonces drawn inside the adapter and not recorded; owns the record type and its canonical description with its member names**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: envelope-key registration verifies a Schnorr proof of possession per key on chain, so the contract's verification must agree with the Rust reference on keys and proofs the reference generated; its Foundry tests read them from a committed vector file, one encoded record per line, each record carrying the inputs the generator chose, the secrets, and the outputs a verifier checks, the public keys and each proof's commitment and response, as Foundry's failure persistence records a run's inputs rather than its generator state (LC-08 at the algebra level, on chain; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/possession_vectors` row; the technical requirements' `<records>` entry for possession; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: for each of the configured count of records, the function draws two uniforms through the randomness family at the length the pairing's scalar sampling bound declares and generates a key pair with its proofs of possession through the key agreement's `generate_keys`
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each record holds the key pair's secrets `x` and `y`, read through `key_pair_components`, each as its 32-byte big-endian encoding; the public keys `pk1` and `pk2`, read through `key_pair_public_keys`, in the pairing's first- and second-group precompile encodings; and the proofs' components `r1`, `z1`, `r2`, and `z2`, read through `possession_components`, the commitments in the precompile encodings and the responses as 32-byte big-endian encodings
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a failed draw and a failed key generation are each returned unchanged in the error arm
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a scalar the pairing encodes to other than 32 bytes is refused with the encoding's length
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the record type's canonical description maps the record to its fields in member order, rebuilds the record from its fields read through the encoding family's canonical-field reader, carrying the reader's refusal unchanged, and states its member names, `x`, `y`, `pk1`, `pk2`, `r1`, `z1`, `r2`, and `z2`, in field order and nothing else about its members; `harness-crypto/generate/solidity` pairs each name with the description's field kind and the borrowed encoder's type name for that kind
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the function reads no clock and touches no filesystem; it names no curve, concrete, tag, or count; the count arrives in its params, the proofs' challenges are the key agreement's own under the tags its declaration carries, and every encoding is the selected pairing's

  * `[✅]`   `role`
    * `[✅]`   App module in the adapter role: a function the Solidity concrete of the harness's generate family owns, beside `render`, `constants`, `group_vectors`, `rejection_vectors`, and `mapping_vectors` beneath the concrete's module
    * `[✅]`   Owns the possession record type and its canonical description with its member names; consumes `VectorCount`, which the generate family's `factory` interface owns and `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/group_vectors` declares there
    * `[✅]`   Does not compute a proof of possession or its challenge; the key agreement generates and proves the keys
    * `[✅]`   Does not encode a record, write a file, or render a library; `harness-crypto/generate/solidity` encodes each record through the encoding factory under its description, writes the vector file, and builds the kind's record declaration from the description's member names, its field kinds, and the borrowed encoder's type names
    * `[✅]`   Does not construct a pairing, key agreement, or randomness source; `harness-crypto/run` and its consumers obtain each through its factory and the generate concrete hands this function the borrowed adapters
    * `[✅]`   Does not carry a commit

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the Solidity verifier recomputes the delivery proof's challenge as the hash-to-scalar mapping of the transcript's canonical encoding under the challenge tag, so its Foundry tests read the reference's transcripts, their encodings, and their challenges from a committed vector file, one encoded record per line; the proof family's challenge function is private to its crate and a proof in the second-group form carries no first messages, so these transcripts stand alone, built by this function over the same public transcript descriptions the proof family encodes through, their context values and purposes taken from the typed contexts it is handed and their points from scalars the function draws and records, as Foundry's failure persistence records a run's inputs rather than its generator state; the proofs' own challenges travel in the proof vectors (CR-09 and CR-11 challenge vectors; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/challenge_vectors` row and its conflict flag on standalone challenge transcripts; the technical requirements' `<records>` entry for challenge; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope and its exit, the challenge vectors mirrored to Solidity and reproduced by the deployed verifier)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a delivery-statement version other than the one the transcript descriptions serve is refused with the version before any other step
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the challenge tag is read from the delivery-proof declaration and admitted as a `DomainTag`
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each record's transcript takes its context values from a clone of the typed context of its relation the payload carries, and its purpose from that context's `purpose`; the function rebuilds no form and refuses no context value
    * `[✅]`   Functional: for each index from zero below the configured count, the function emits a mint record then a transfer record; a mint transcript holds the mint context, the mint purpose, and eleven points, its buyer keys, envelope, and first messages; a transfer transcript holds the transfer context, the transfer purpose, and eighteen points, its seller keys, buyer keys, old and new envelopes, and first messages
    * `[✅]`   Functional: every point is drawn as a uniform of the length the pairing's scalar sampling bound declares, sampled into a scalar through that bound, and multiplied onto its group's generator; a record's draws are each drawn scalar's 32-byte big-endian encoding, concatenated in the order the transcript's description lists its group fields
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each record holds its purpose's code, its draws, the bytes the encoding family encodes its transcript to under the transcript's description, and the 32-byte big-endian encoding of the scalar `hash_to_scalar` returns for those bytes under the challenge tag
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a refused tag, a failed draw, a failed sampling, a failed encoding, and a failed hash are each returned unchanged in the error arm
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the record type's canonical description maps the record to its fields in member order, rebuilds the record from its fields read through the encoding family's canonical-field reader, carrying the reader's refusal unchanged, and states its member names, `purpose`, `draws`, `encoding`, and `challenge`, in field order and nothing else about its members; `harness-crypto/generate/solidity` pairs each name with the description's field kind and the borrowed encoder's type name for that kind
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the function reads no clock and touches no filesystem; it names no curve, concrete, form, tag, count, chain, purpose, or context value; the count and version arrive in its params, the tag in the declaration, the typed contexts, each carrying its purpose, in its payload, and every encoding is the selected pairing's and the encoding family's
